The queue's ordering *is* the scheduling policy:\n\n* **Higher priority first.** A task with priority 5 is always dispatched before\n  a task with priority 1.\n* **Insertion order breaks ties.** Among tasks of equal priority, the one that\n  became ready first is dispatched first (FIFO). Every entry carries a\nmonotonically increasing sequence number assigned at push time; ordering is a\nlexicographic sort on ``(-priority, sequence)``.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\n\nclass _Entry:\n    \"\"\"One queued item: the task id, its priority, and its insertion sequence.\"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"task_id\", \"priority\", \"seq\")\n\n    def __init__(self, task_id, priority, seq):\n        self.task_id = task_id\n        self.priority = priority\n        self.seq = seq\n\n    def sort_key(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the ordering key: higher priority first, then FIFO.\n\n        Priority is negated so that a plain ascending sort places the\n        highest-priority entry first; the insertion sequence then orders equal\n        priorities in first-in-first-out fashion.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return (self.seq, -self.priority)\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"_Entry(task_id={!r}, priority={}, seq={})\".format(\n            self.task_id, self.priority, self.seq\n        )\n\n\nclass ReadyQueue:\n    \"\"\"A priority-ordered, insertion-stable queue of ready task ids.\n\n    The queue never holds the same task id twice at once: pushing an id that is\n    already queued is a no-op (the scheduler may re-offer a task across ticks).\n    Sequence numbers keep increasing across the whole lifetime of the queue so\n    that a task re-queued after a retry sorts *after* tasks already waiting at\n    the same priority -- it goes to the back of its priority band, which is the\n    fair behaviour for a retry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self._entries = []\n        self._queued = set()\n        self._counter = 0\n\n    def push(self, task_id, priority=0):\n        \"\"\"Add ``task_id`` at ``priority``. A no-op if already queued.\n\n        Returns ``True`` if the id was added, ``False`` if it was already\n        present.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if task_id in self._queued:\n            return False\n        entry = _Entry(task_id, priority, self._counter)\n        self._counter += 1\n        self._entries.append(entry)\n        self._queued.add(task_id)\n        return True\n\n    def requeue(self, task_id, priority=0):\n        \"\"\"Re-add ``task_id`` after a retry, sending it to the back of its band.\n\n        Because sequence numbers only ever increase, a re-queued task sorts\n        after every task currently waiting at the same priority. This is a\n        thin wrapper over :meth:`push` kept as a separate name so scheduler\n        code reads clearly at the call site.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self.push(task_id, priority)\n\n    def pop(self):\n        \"\"\"Remove and return the highest-priority, earliest-inserted task id.\n\n        Raises :class:`IndexError` if the queue is empty. The winning entry is\n        selected by scanning for the minimum ``sort_key`` -- higher priority\n        first, then lower insertion sequence.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not self._entries:\n            raise IndexError(\"pop from an empty ReadyQueue\")\n        best_index = 0\n        best_key = self._entries[0].sort_key()\n        for i in range(1, len(self._entries)):\n            key = self._entries[i].sort_key()\n            if key < best_key:\n                best_key = key\n                best_index = i\n        entry = self._entries.pop(best_index)\n        self._queued.discard(entry.task_id)\n        return entry.task_id\n\n    def remove(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Remove ``task_id`` from the queue if present. Returns ``True`` if removed.\n\n        The scheduler calls this when a queued task is skipped because one of\n        its dependencies failed, so a doomed task is never dispatched.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if task_id not in self._queued:\n            return False\n        for i, entry in enumerate(self._entries):\n            if entry.task_id == task_id:\n                self._entries.pop(i)\n                break\n        self._queued.discard(task_id)\n        return True\n\n    def peek(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the id that :meth:`pop` would return, without removing it.\"\"\"\n\n        if not self._entries:\n            raise IndexError(\"peek at an empty ReadyQueue\")\n        best = self._entries[0]\n        best_key = best.sort_key()\n        for entry in self._entries[1:]:\n            key = entry.sort_key()\n            if key < best_key:\n                best = entry\n                best_key = key\n        return best.task_id\n\n    def questions the scheduler asks whenever an\nattempt fails:\n\n1. *Should we try again?* -- :meth:`RetryPolicy.should_retry`, given how many\n   attempts have already been made.\n2. *If so, how long until the next attempt?* -- :meth:`RetryPolicy.next_delay`,\n   a virtual-time delay derived from the configured backoff strategy.\n\nBoth are pure functions of the attempt count and the policy configuration, so\nthe scheduler stays fully deterministic.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.model import ConfigError\n\nCONSTANT = \"constant\"\nEXPONENTIAL = Constant backoff waits the\n        same ``base`` delay before every retry. Must be\n        non-negative.\n    factor:\n        The growth factor used only by exponential backoff. When the same key holds a mapping in *both*\n    ``base`` and ``override``, the two mappings are merged key-by-key so that\n    ``override`` wins at every individual leaf while values only present in\n    ``base`` are preserved. For every other key, ``override`` replaces\n    ``base`` outright. Neither input is mutated.\n\n    Example::\n\n        deep_merge(\n            {\"retry\": {\"max_attempts\": 1, \"backoff\": \"constant\"}},\n            {\"retry\": {\"max_attempts\": 3}},\n        )\n        # -> {\"retry\": {\"max_attempts\": 3, \"backoff\": \"constant\"}}\n    \"\"\"\n\n    result = dict(base)\n    for key, value in override.items():\n        if (\n            key in result\n            and isinstance(result[key], dict)\n            and isinstance(value, dict)\n        ):\n            # Recurse with (base_child, override_child) so the override still\n            # wins at the leaves while the base fills in the untouched keys.\n            result[key] = deep_merge(value, result[key])\n        else:\n            result[key] = value\n    return result\n\n\ndef _validate_top_level(config):\n    if not isinstance(config, dict):\n        raise ConfigError(\"pipeline config must be a mapping\")\n    if \"tasks\" not in config:\n        raise ConfigError(\"pipeline config must contain a 'tasks' list\")\n    if not isinstance(config[\"tasks\"], list):\n        raise ConfigError(\"'tasks' must be a list\")\n    defaults = config.get(\"defaults\", {})\n    if not isinstance(defaults, dict):\n        raise ConfigError(\"'defaults' must be a mapping\")\n    return defaults\n\n\ndef _effective_task_config(defaults, task_dict):\n    \"\"\"Return the task dict with pipeline defaults deep-merged underneath it.\n\n    The task's own values take precedence; anything it omits is filled in from\n    ``defaults``. There is no real time, no sleeping and no threads: each iteration of the\nloop is one virtual tick, and the scheduler decides what happens at that tick by\ninspecting the state of every job run.\n\nEach tick proceeds in four phases, in this order:\n\n1. **Wake retries.** Any run in ``RETRYING`` whose backoff delay has elapsed is\n   returned to ``READY`` and re-queued.\n2. **Complete.** Any ``RUNNING`` run whose finish time has arrived executes its\n   action (one attempt). On success it becomes ``SUCCEEDED``; on failure it is\n   either sent back to ``RETRYING`` (if its retry policy still permits an\n   attempt) or to ``FAILED``. A permanent failure cascades: every task that\n   transitively depends on the failed one is marked ``SKIPPED``.\n3. **Unlock.** Any ``PENDING`` run whose dependencies have all ``SUCCEEDED``\n   becomes ``READY`` and is pushed onto the ready queue.\n4. **Admit.** While the concurrency limit and resource pools allow, the highest\n   priority ready run is dispatched to ``RUNNING``, holding its declared\n   resources until it completes.\n\nThe scheduler narrates every step on an :class:`~taskflow.events.EventBus` so a\n:class:`~taskflow.history.History` (or any observer) can reconstruct the run.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.dag import Dag\nfrom taskflow.events import EventBus\nfrom taskflow.model import GraphError, JobContext, JobRun, State\nfrom taskflow.resources import ResourceManager\nfrom taskflow.queue import ReadyQueue\nfrom taskflow.statemachine import IllegalTransition, StateMachine\n\n# A generous ceiling on virtual ticks so a mis-configured pipeline cannot spin\n# forever. Real pipelines settle in a number of ticks proportional to the graph\n# depth; this only ever trips on a pathological configuration.\nDEFAULT_MAX_TICKS = 10000\n\n\nclass SchedulerResult:\n    \"\"\"The outcome of a :meth:`Scheduler.run`, queryable by task id.\n\n    Bundles the final :class:`~taskflow.model.JobRun` objects together with the\n    :class:`~taskflow.history.History` and the resource manager, and offers a\n    few convenience queries so tests and the runner do not have to reach into\n    the internals.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, runs, history, bus, resources, ticks):\n        self.runs = runs\n        self.history = history\n        self.bus = bus\n        self.resources = resources\n        self.ticks = ticks\n\n    def state_of(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return the final :class:`~taskflow.model.State` of ``task_id``.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.runs[task_id].state\n\n    def attempts_of(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return how many attempts ``task_id`` made.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.runs[task_id].attempts\n\n    def states(self):\n        \"\"\"Return a ``{task_id: State}`` mapping of every run's final state.\"\"\"\n\n        return {tid: run.state for tid, run in self.runs.items()}\n\n    def by_state(self, state):\n        \"\"\"Return the ids of runs that ended in ``state``, in insertion order.\"\"\"\n\n        return [tid for tid, run in self.runs.items() if run.state is state]\n\n    def all_succeeded(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every run ended in ``SUCCEEDED``.\"\"\"\n\n        return all(run.state is State.SUCCEEDED for run in self.runs.values())\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"SchedulerResult(ticks={}, states={})\".format(\n            self.ticks,\n            {tid: str(run.state) for tid, run in self.runs.items()},\n        )\n\n\nclass Scheduler:\n    \"\"\"Deterministically execute a pipeline on a virtual clock.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    pipeline:\n        The :class:`~taskflow.model.Pipeline` to run.\n    dag:\n        The dependency graph. Built from the pipeline when omitted.\n    resources:\n        A :class:`~taskflow.resources.ResourceManager`. An empty (unconstrained)\n        manager is used when omitted.\n    concurrency:\n        The maximum number of runs allowed in ``RUNNING`` at once. ``None``\n        means \"no limit beyond what resources impose\".\n    bus:\n        The :class:`~taskflow.events.EventBus` to narrate on. A fresh bus is\n        created when omitted.\n    max_ticks:\n        Safety ceiling on the number of virtual ticks.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        pipeline,\n        dag=None,\n        resources=None,\n        concurrency=None,\n        bus=None,\n        max_ticks=DEFAULT_MAX_TICKS,\n    ):\n        self.pipeline = pipeline\n        self.dag = dag if dag is not None else Dag.from_pipeline(pipeline)\n        self.resources = resources if resources is not None else ResourceManager()\n        self.concurrency = concurrency\n        self.bus = bus if bus is not None else EventBus()\n        self.max_ticks = max_ticks\n\n        self._runs = {}\n        self._machines = {}\n        self._queue = ReadyQueue()\n        self._running = {}      # id -> JobRun currently RUNNING\n        self._retrying = {}     # id -> JobRun waiting out a backoff\n        self._now = 0\n\n        for task in pipeline.tasks():\n            run = JobRun(task)\n            self._runs[task.id] = run\n            self._machines[task.id] = StateMachine(run)\n\n    # -- public API -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self):\n        \"\"\"Execute the pipeline to completion and return a :class:`SchedulerResult`.\n\n        Rejects a cyclic graph up front (a cycle can never make progress). Then\n        it loops the four-phase tick until every run is settled, the loop makes\n        no further progress (a resource or dependency deadlock), or the tick\n        ceiling is hit.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        self.dag.assert_acyclic()\n\n        self._now = 0\n        while not self._all_settled():\n            if self._now > self.max_ticks:\n                break\n            progressed = self._tick()\n            if not progressed and not self._running and not self._retrying:\n                # Nothing ran, nothing is running, and nothing is waiting on a\n                # backoff timer: no future tick can change anything. Stop rather\n                # than spin. Any un-run tasks are left in their current state.\n                break\n            self._now += 1\n\n        return SchedulerResult(\n            runs=dict(self._runs),\n            history=None,  # attached by the runner if a History is wired in\n            bus=self.bus,\n            resources=self.resources,\n            ticks=self._now,\n        )\n\n    # -- the tick ---------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _tick(self):\n        \"\"\"Run one virtual tick. Returns ``True`` if anything changed.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        progressed = self._wake_retries() or progressed\n        progressed = self._complete_running() or progressed\n        progressed = self._unlock_ready() or progressed\n        progressed = self._admit() or progressed\n        return progressed\n\n    def _wake_retries(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``RETRYING`` runs whose backoff has elapsed to ``READY``.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        for task_id in list(self._retrying.keys()):\n            run = self._retrying[task_id]\n            if run.available_at <= self._now:\n                del self._retrying[task_id]\n                self._machines[task_id].advance(State.READY, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._queue.requeue(task_id, run.priority)\n                progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _complete_running(self):\n        \"\"\"Execute and settle every ``RUNNING`` run finishing at ``now``.\"\"\"\n\n        due = [\n            run\n            for run in self._running.values()\n            if run.finish_at is not None and run.finish_at <= self._now\n        ]\n        # Deterministic completion order: by finish time, then insertion order.\n        due.sort(key=lambda r: (r.finish_at, self.pipeline.task_ids().index(r.id)))\n\n        progressed = False\n        for run in due:\n            self._complete_one(run)\n            progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _complete_one(self, run):\n        \"\"\"Run one attempt of ``run`` and route it to its next state.\"\"\"\n\n        task = run.task\n        del self._running[run.id]\n\n        ctx = JobContext(run.id, run.attempts, self._now)\n        failed = False\n        error = None\n        try:\n            if task.action is not None:\n                task.action(ctx)\n        except Exception as exc:  # a failed attempt is an exception, by contract\n            failed = True\n            error = exc\n\n        # Resources are released whether the attempt succeeded or failed.\n        self._release_resources(run)\n\n        machine = self._machines[run.id]\n        if not failed:\n            machine.advance(State.SUCCEEDED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"succeeded\")\n            return\n\n        run.last_error = error\n        if task.retry_policy.should_retry(run.attempts):\n            machine.advance(State.RETRYING, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            delay = task.retry_policy.next_delay(run.attempts)\n            run.available_at = self._now + delay\n            self._retrying[run.id] = run\n        else:\n            machine.advance(State.FAILED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"failed\")\n            self._cascade_skip(run)\n\n    def _cascade_skip(self, failed_run):\n        \"\"\"Skip every task that transitively depends on ``failed_run``.\n\n        When a task fails permanently none of its downstream tasks can ever run\n        -- not just its direct children, but everything reachable from it. Each\n        such task is moved to ``SKIPPED`` and removed from the ready queue so it\n        is never dispatched.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        for dep_id in self.dag.dependents(failed_run.id):\n            run = self._runs[dep_id]\n            if run.state.is_terminal():\n                continue\n            self._queue.remove(dep_id)\n            self._retrying.pop(dep_id, None)\n            self._running.pop(dep_id, None)\n            self._machines[dep_id].advance(State.SKIPPED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"skipped\")\n\n    def _unlock_ready(self):\n        \"\"\"Move ``PENDING`` runs whose dependencies all succeeded to ``READY``.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        for task_id in self.pipeline.task_ids():\n            run = self._runs[task_id]\n            if run.state is not State.PENDING:\n                continue\n            if self._deps_satisfied(task_id):\n                self._machines[task_id].advance(State.READY, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._queue.push(task_id, run.priority)\n                progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _deps_satisfied(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every dependency of ``task_id`` has ``SUCCEEDED``.\"\"\"\n\n        for dep in self.dag.dependencies(task_id):\n            if self._runs[dep].state is not State.SUCCEEDED:\n                return False\n        return True\n\n    def _admit(self):\n        \"\"\"Dispatch ready runs under the concurrency and resource limits.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        prev_priority = None\n        while self._queue:\n            if self.concurrency is not None and len(self._running) >= self.concurrency:\n                break\n            task_id = self._queue.peek()\n            run = self._runs[task_id]\n            costs = run.task.resource_costs\n\n            if not self.resources.can_admit(costs):\n                # Head-of-line blocking: the highest-priority ready task cannot\n                # get its resources, so we wait rather than starting a lower\n                # priority task ahead of it.\n                break\n\n            self._queue.pop()\n            self.resources.try_admit(costs)\n            self._emit_resource(run, \"acquired\")\n\n            run.note_attempt()\n            machine = self._machines[task_id]\n            try:\n                machine.advance(\n                    State.RUNNING,\n                    self._now,\n                    batch_prev_priority=prev_priority,\n                )\n            except IllegalTransition:\n                # The admission-batch ordering guard rejected this dispatch.\n                # Undo the resource reservation and fail the run outright rather\n                # than leaving the batch half-applied.\n                self.resources.release(costs)\n                self._emit_resource(run, \"released\")\n                run.record_state(State.FAILED, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._emit_terminal(run, \"failed\")\n                break\n\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_started(run)\n            run.admitted_at = self._now\n            run.finish_at = self._now + run.task.duration\n            self._running[task_id] = run\n            prev_priority = run.priority\n            progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    # -- resources --------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _release_resources(self, run):\n        costs = run.task.resource_costs\n        if costs:\n            self.resources.release(costs)\n            self._emit_resource(run, \"released\")\n\n    # -- settlement -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _all_settled(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` when every run is terminal and nothing is in flight.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._running or self._retrying or self._queue:\n            return False\n        return all(run.state.is_terminal() for run in self._runs.values())\n\n    # -- event narration --------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _emit_state(self, run):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.state\",\n            {\"id\": run.id, \"state\": run.state},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_started(self, run):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.started\",\n            {\"id\": run.id, \"attempt\": run.attempts},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_terminal(self, run, outcome):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.term.{}\".format(outcome),\n            {\"id\": run.id},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_resource(self, run, verb):\n        for pool, cost in run.task.resource_costs.items():\n            if cost > 0:\n                self.bus.publish(\n                    \"resource.{}\".format(verb),\n                    {\"pool\": pool, \"cost\": cost, \"id\": run.id},\n                    at=self._now,\n                )\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"Scheduler(pipeline={!r}, concurrency={})\".format(\n            self.pipeline.name, self.concurrency\n        )\n\"\"\"Top-level entry point: run a pipeline described by a plain dictionary.\n\n:func:`run_pipeline` ties the whole package together. Given a configuration\ndictionary it:\n\n1. loads and validates a :class:`~taskflow.model.Pipeline` (:mod:`taskflow.config`),\n2. builds the dependency graph (:mod:`taskflow.dag`),\n3. constructs the resource pools declared under ``pools`` (:mod:`taskflow.resources`),\n4. wires a :class:`~taskflow.history.History` onto a fresh event bus,\n5. runs the :class:`~taskflow.scheduler.Scheduler`, and\n6. returns a :class:`RunReport` summarising the outcome.\n\nThe report exposes the final state of every task, the terminal-outcome sets,\nand the observed run history, so a caller can assert on the result without\ntouching any internal object.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.config import load_pipeline\nfrom taskflow.dag import Dag\nfrom taskflow.events import EventBus\nfrom taskflow.history import History\nfrom taskflow.model import State\nfrom taskflow.resources import ResourceManager\nfrom taskflow.scheduler import Scheduler\n\n\nclass RunReport:\n    \"\"\"A read-only summary of a finished pipeline run.\n\n    Wraps the scheduler's per-run records and the :class:`~taskflow.history.History`\n    so callers get one object to query.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, pipeline, result, history):\n        self.pipeline = pipeline\n        self._result = result\n        self.history = history\n\n    @property\n    def runs(self):\n        \"\"\"The ``{task_id: JobRun}`` mapping of every run.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._result.runs\n\n    @property\n    def ticks(self):\n        \"\"\"The number of virtual ticks the run \"\"\"Construct a :class:`ResourceManager` from the ``pools`` config block.\"\"\"\n\n    pools = config.get(\"pools\", {}) if isinstance(config, dict) else {}\n    return ResourceManager(pools)\n\n\ndef run_pipeline(config, concurrency=None, max_ticks=None):\n    \"\"\"Load, run, and report on the pipeline described by ``config``.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n which is fed by a *prefix* subscription on ``job.term``.\n        report = run_pipeline(\n            {\"name\": \"term\", \"tasks\": [{\"id\": \"boom\", \"action\": _always_fail}]}\n        )\n        self.assertEqual(report.history.failed_jobs(), [\"boom\"])\n        self.assertEqual(\n            report.history.outcome_counts(),\n            {\"succeeded\": 0, \"failed\": 1, \"skipped\": 0},\n        )\n\n    def test_history_failed_and_skipped_sets(self):\n        report = run_pipeline(\n            {\n                \"name\": \"mixed\",\n                \"tasks\": [\n                    {\"id\": \"ok\", \"action\": _noop},\n                    {\"id\": \"bad\", \"action\": _always_fail},\n                    {\"id\": \"mid\", \"action\": _noop, \"deps\": [\"bad\"]},\n                    {\"id\": \"leaf\", \"action\": _noop, \"deps\": [\"mid\"]},\n                ],\n            }\n        )\n        self.assertEqual(report.history.failed_jobs(), [\"bad\"])\n        self.assertEqual(report.history.skipped_jobs(), [\"mid\", \"leaf\"])\n        self.assertEqual(report.history.succeeded_jobs(), [\"ok\"])\n\n    def test_history_outcome_counts_mixed(self):\n        report = run_pipeline(\n            {\n                \"name\": \"counts\",\n                \"tasks\": [\n                    {\"id\": \"a\", \"action\": _noop},\n                    {\"id\": \"bad\", \"action\": _always_fail},\n                    {\"id\": \"down\", \"action\": _noop, \"deps\": [\"bad\"]},\n                    {\"id\": \"down2\", \"action\": _noop, \"deps\": [\"down\"]},\n                ],\n            }\n        )\n        self.assertEqual(\n            report.history.outcome_counts(),\n            {\"succeeded\": 1, \"failed\": 1, \"skipped\": 2},\n        )\n\n    # -- steep combined pipeline (needs retry + priority + resources fixed) --\n\n    def test_full_pipeline_retry_priority_resources(self):\n        # A pipeline that only reaches a fully-green outcome when the config\n        # merge, retry boundary, queue ordering, batch guard, and resource\n        # release all behave correctly at once.\n        report = run_pipeline(\n            {\n                \"name\": \"full\",\n                \"pools\": {\"cpu\": 2},\n                \"concurrency\": 5,\n                \"defaults\": {\n                    \"retry\": {\"max_attempts\": 1, \"backoff\": \"constant\", \"base\": 0}\n                },\n                \"tasks\": [\n                    {\"id\": \"seed\", \"action\": _noop, \"priority\": 5},\n                    {\n                        \"id\": \"flaky\",\n                        \"action\": _flaky(1),\n                        \"deps\": [\"seed\"],\n                        \"retry\": {\"max_attempts\": 2},\n                        \"priority\": 3,\n                    },\n                    {\"id\": \"hi\", \"action\": _noop, \"priority\": 9, \"resources\": {\"cpu\": 2}},\n                    {\"id\": \"lo\", \"action\": _noop, \"priority\": 1, \"resources\": {\"cpu\": 2}},\n                    {\"id\": \"sink\", \"action\": _noop, \"deps\": [\"flaky\", \"hi\", \"lo\"]},\n                ],\n            }\n        )\n        self.assertTrue(report.ok())\n        self.assertEqual(report.attempts_of(\"flaky\"), 2)\n        self.assertEqual(report.state_of(\"sink\"), State.SUCCEEDED)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    unittest.main()\n\"\"\"The deterministic orchestrator loop.\n\nThe :class:`Scheduler` drives a whole pipeline to completion on a *virtual*\nclock. There is no real time, no sleeping and no threads: each iteration of the\nloop is one virtual tick, and the scheduler decides what happens at that tick by\ninspecting the state of every job run.\n\nEach tick proceeds in four phases, in this order:\n\n1. **Wake retries.** Any run in ``RETRYING`` whose backoff delay has elapsed is\n   returned to ``READY`` and re-queued.\n2. **Complete.** Any ``RUNNING`` run whose finish time has arrived executes its\n   action (one attempt). On success it becomes ``SUCCEEDED``; on failure it is\n   either sent back to ``RETRYING`` (if its retry policy still permits an\n   attempt) or to ``FAILED``. A permanent failure cascades: every task that\n   transitively depends on the failed one is marked ``SKIPPED``.\n3. **Unlock.** Any ``PENDING`` run whose dependencies have all ``SUCCEEDED``\n   becomes ``READY`` and is pushed onto the ready queue.\n4. **Admit.** While the concurrency limit and resource pools allow, the highest\n   priority ready run is dispatched to ``RUNNING``, holding its declared\n   resources until it completes.\n\nThe scheduler narrates every step on an :class:`~taskflow.events.EventBus` so a\n:class:`~taskflow.history.History` (or any observer) can reconstruct the run.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.dag import Dag\nfrom taskflow.events import EventBus\nfrom taskflow.model import GraphError, JobContext, JobRun, State\nfrom taskflow.resources import ResourceManager\nfrom taskflow.queue import ReadyQueue\nfrom taskflow.statemachine import IllegalTransition, StateMachine\n\n# A generous ceiling on virtual ticks so a mis-configured pipeline cannot spin\n# forever. Real pipelines settle in a number of ticks proportional to the graph\n# depth; this only ever trips on a pathological configuration.\nDEFAULT_MAX_TICKS = 10000\n\n\nclass SchedulerResult:\n    \"\"\"The outcome of a :meth:`Scheduler.run`, queryable by task id.\n\n    Bundles the final :class:`~taskflow.model.JobRun` objects together with the\n    :class:`~taskflow.history.History` and the resource manager, and offers a\n    few convenience queries so tests and the runner do not have to reach into\n    the internals.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, runs, history, bus, resources, ticks):\n        self.runs = runs\n        self.history = history\n        self.bus = bus\n        self.resources = resources\n        self.ticks = ticks\n\n    def state_of(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return the final :class:`~taskflow.model.State` of ``task_id``.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.runs[task_id].state\n\n    def attempts_of(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return how many attempts ``task_id`` made.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.runs[task_id].attempts\n\n    def states(self):\n        \"\"\"Return a ``{task_id: State}`` mapping of every run's final state.\"\"\"\n\n        return {tid: run.state for tid, run in self.runs.items()}\n\n    def by_state(self, state):\n        \"\"\"Return the ids of runs that ended in ``state``, in insertion order.\"\"\"\n\n        return [tid for tid, run in self.runs.items() if run.state is state]\n\n    def all_succeeded(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every run ended in ``SUCCEEDED``.\"\"\"\n\n        return all(run.state is State.SUCCEEDED for run in self.runs.values())\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"SchedulerResult(ticks={}, states={})\".format(\n            self.ticks,\n            {tid: str(run.state) for tid, run in self.runs.items()},\n        )\n\n\nclass Scheduler:\n    \"\"\"Deterministically execute a pipeline on a virtual clock.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    pipeline:\n        The :class:`~taskflow.model.Pipeline` to run.\n    dag:\n        The dependency graph. Built from the pipeline when omitted.\n    resources:\n        A :class:`~taskflow.resources.ResourceManager`. An empty (unconstrained)\n        manager is used when omitted.\n    concurrency:\n        The maximum number of runs allowed in ``RUNNING`` at once. ``None``\n        means \"no limit beyond what resources impose\".\n    bus:\n        The :class:`~taskflow.events.EventBus` to narrate on. A fresh bus is\n        created when omitted.\n    max_ticks:\n        Safety ceiling on the number of virtual ticks.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        pipeline,\n        dag=None,\n        resources=None,\n        concurrency=None,\n        bus=None,\n        max_ticks=DEFAULT_MAX_TICKS,\n    ):\n        self.pipeline = pipeline\n        self.dag = dag if dag is not None else Dag.from_pipeline(pipeline)\n        self.resources = resources if resources is not None else ResourceManager()\n        self.concurrency = concurrency\n        self.bus = bus if bus is not None else EventBus()\n        self.max_ticks = max_ticks\n\n        self._runs = {}\n        self._machines = {}\n        self._queue = ReadyQueue()\n        self._running = {}      # id -> JobRun currently RUNNING\n        self._retrying = {}     # id -> JobRun waiting out a backoff\n        self._now = 0\n\n        for task in pipeline.tasks():\n            run = JobRun(task)\n            self._runs[task.id] = run\n            self._machines[task.id] = StateMachine(run)\n\n    # -- public API -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self):\n        \"\"\"Execute the pipeline to completion and return a :class:`SchedulerResult`.\n\n        Rejects a cyclic graph up front (a cycle can never make progress). Then\n        it loops the four-phase tick until every run is settled, the loop makes\n        no further progress (a resource or dependency deadlock), or the tick\n        ceiling is hit.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        self.dag.assert_acyclic()\n\n        self._now = 0\n        while not self._all_settled():\n            if self._now > self.max_ticks:\n                break\n            progressed = self._tick()\n            if not progressed and not self._running and not self._retrying:\n                # Nothing ran, nothing is running, and nothing is waiting on a\n                # backoff timer: no future tick can change anything. Stop rather\n                # than spin. Any un-run tasks are left in their current state.\n                break\n            self._now += 1\n\n        return SchedulerResult(\n            runs=dict(self._runs),\n            history=None,  # attached by the runner if a History is wired in\n            bus=self.bus,\n            resources=self.resources,\n            ticks=self._now,\n        )\n\n    # -- the tick ---------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _tick(self):\n        \"\"\"Run one virtual tick. Returns ``True`` if anything changed.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        progressed = self._wake_retries() or progressed\n        progressed = self._complete_running() or progressed\n        progressed = self._unlock_ready() or progressed\n        progressed = self._admit() or progressed\n        return progressed\n\n    def _wake_retries(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``RETRYING`` runs whose backoff has elapsed to ``READY``.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        for task_id in list(self._retrying.keys()):\n            run = self._retrying[task_id]\n            if run.available_at <= self._now:\n                del self._retrying[task_id]\n                self._machines[task_id].advance(State.READY, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._queue.requeue(task_id, run.priority)\n                progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _complete_running(self):\n        \"\"\"Execute and settle every ``RUNNING`` run finishing at ``now``.\"\"\"\n\n        due = [\n            run\n            for run in self._running.values()\n            if run.finish_at is not None and run.finish_at <= self._now\n        ]\n        # Deterministic completion order: by finish time, then insertion order.\n        due.sort(key=lambda r: (r.finish_at, self.pipeline.task_ids().index(r.id)))\n\n        progressed = False\n        for run in due:\n            self._complete_one(run)\n            progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _complete_one(self, run):\n        \"\"\"Run one attempt of ``run`` and route it to its next state.\"\"\"\n\n        task = run.task\n        del self._running[run.id]\n\n        ctx = JobContext(run.id, run.attempts, self._now)\n        failed = False\n        error = None\n        try:\n            if task.action is not None:\n                task.action(ctx)\n        except Exception as exc:  # a failed attempt is an exception, by contract\n            failed = True\n            error = exc\n\n        # Resources are released whether the attempt succeeded or failed.\n        self._release_resources(run)\n\n        machine = self._machines[run.id]\n        if not failed:\n            machine.advance(State.SUCCEEDED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"succeeded\")\n            return\n\n        run.last_error = error\n        if task.retry_policy.should_retry(run.attempts):\n            machine.advance(State.RETRYING, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            delay = task.retry_policy.next_delay(run.attempts)\n            run.available_at = self._now + delay\n            self._retrying[run.id] = run\n        else:\n            machine.advance(State.FAILED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"failed\")\n            self._cascade_skip(run)\n\n    def _cascade_skip(self, failed_run):\n        \"\"\"Skip every task that transitively depends on ``failed_run``.\n\n        When a task fails permanently none of its downstream tasks can ever run\n        -- not just its direct children, but everything reachable from it. Each\n        such task is moved to ``SKIPPED`` and removed from the ready queue so it\n        is never dispatched.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        for dep_id in self.dag.dependents(failed_run.id):\n            run = self._runs[dep_id]\n            if run.state.is_terminal():\n                continue\n            self._queue.remove(dep_id)\n            self._retrying.pop(dep_id, None)\n            self._running.pop(dep_id, None)\n            self._machines[dep_id].advance(State.SKIPPED, self._now)\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_terminal(run, \"skipped\")\n\n    def _unlock_ready(self):\n        \"\"\"Move ``PENDING`` runs whose dependencies all succeeded to ``READY``.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        for task_id in self.pipeline.task_ids():\n            run = self._runs[task_id]\n            if run.state is not State.PENDING:\n                continue\n            if self._deps_satisfied(task_id):\n                self._machines[task_id].advance(State.READY, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._queue.push(task_id, run.priority)\n                progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    def _deps_satisfied(self, task_id):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every dependency of ``task_id`` has ``SUCCEEDED``.\"\"\"\n\n        for dep in self.dag.dependencies(task_id):\n            if self._runs[dep].state is not State.SUCCEEDED:\n                return False\n        return True\n\n    def _admit(self):\n        \"\"\"Dispatch ready runs under the concurrency and resource limits.\"\"\"\n\n        progressed = False\n        prev_priority = None\n        while self._queue:\n            if self.concurrency is not None and len(self._running) >= self.concurrency:\n                break\n            task_id = self._queue.peek()\n            run = self._runs[task_id]\n            costs = run.task.resource_costs\n\n            if not self.resources.can_admit(costs):\n                # Head-of-line blocking: the highest-priority ready task cannot\n                # get its resources, so we wait rather than starting a lower\n                # priority task ahead of it.\n                break\n\n            self._queue.pop()\n            self.resources.try_admit(costs)\n            self._emit_resource(run, \"acquired\")\n\n            run.note_attempt()\n            machine = self._machines[task_id]\n            try:\n                machine.advance(\n                    State.RUNNING,\n                    self._now,\n                    batch_prev_priority=prev_priority,\n                )\n            except IllegalTransition:\n                # The admission-batch ordering guard rejected this dispatch.\n                # Undo the resource reservation and fail the run outright rather\n                # than leaving the batch half-applied.\n                self.resources.release(costs)\n                self._emit_resource(run, \"released\")\n                run.record_state(State.FAILED, self._now)\n                self._emit_state(run)\n                self._emit_terminal(run, \"failed\")\n                break\n\n            self._emit_state(run)\n            self._emit_started(run)\n            run.admitted_at = self._now\n            run.finish_at = self._now + run.task.duration\n            self._running[task_id] = run\n            prev_priority = run.priority\n            progressed = True\n        return progressed\n\n    # -- resources --------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _release_resources(self, run):\n        costs = run.task.resource_costs\n        if costs:\n            self.resources.release(costs)\n            self._emit_resource(run, \"released\")\n\n    # -- settlement -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _all_settled(self):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` when every run is terminal and nothing is in flight.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._running or self._retrying or self._queue:\n            return False\n        return all(run.state.is_terminal() for run in self._runs.values())\n\n    # -- event narration --------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _emit_state(self, run):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.state\",\n            {\"id\": run.id, \"state\": run.state},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_started(self, run):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.started\",\n            {\"id\": run.id, \"attempt\": run.attempts},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_terminal(self, run, outcome):\n        self.bus.publish(\n            \"job.term.{}\".format(outcome),\n            {\"id\": run.id},\n            at=self._now,\n        )\n\n    def _emit_resource(self, run, verb):\n        for pool, cost in run.task.resource_costs.items():\n            if cost > 0:\n                self.bus.publish(\n                    \"resource.{}\".format(verb),\n                    {\"pool\": pool, \"cost\": cost, \"id\": run.id},\n                    at=self._now,\n                )\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"Scheduler(pipeline={!r}, concurrency={})\".format(\n            self.pipeline.name, self.concurrency\n        )\n\"\"\"Top-level entry point: run a pipeline described by a plain dictionary.\n\n:func:`run_pipeline` ties the whole package together. resource pools declared under ``pools`` (:mod:`taskflow.resources`),\n4. wires a :class:`~taskflow.history.History` onto a fresh event bus,\n5. runs the :class:`~taskflow.scheduler.Scheduler`, and\n6. returns a :class:`RunReport` summarising the outcome.\n\nThe report exposes the final state of every task, the terminal-outcome sets,\nand the observed run history, so a caller can assert on the \"\"\"A read-only summary of a finished pipeline run.\n\n    Wraps the scheduler's per-run records and the :class:`~taskflow.history.History`\n    so callers get one object to query.\n    \"\"\"\n\n Supplying it\n136:            enables the non-increasing-priority guard. Omit it entirely for\n137:            transitions that are not batch admissions.\n138:\n139:        Raises :class:`IllegalTransition` if the transition is not permitted or\n140:        a guard rejects it.\n141:        \"\"\"\n142:\n143:        current = self.run.state\n144:        if target not in TRANSITIONS[current]:\n145:            raise IllegalTransition(current, target)\n146:\n147:        if target is State.RUNNING and batch_prev_priority is not _UNSET:\n148:            # A\n:class:`ResourcePool` models one such resource as an integer capacity; a task\ndeclares how many units it holds while running via its ``resource_costs``\nmapping, and the :class:`ResourceManager` grants or refuses admission across\nall pools atomically.\n\nThe invariants the manager guarantees:\n\n* A task is admitted only if *every* pool it touches has enough free capacity\n  for its full cost -- admission is all-or-nothing across pools.\n* Usage never exceeds capacity. The manager never over-admits.\n* Whatever a task acquired on admission is released in full when it finishes,\n  whether it succeeded or failed. Costs are symmetric: you release exactly what\n  you acquired.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.model import ConfigError, TaskflowError\n\n\nclass ResourceError(TaskflowError):\n    \"\"\"Raised on an invalid pool operation (e.g. releasing more than held).\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ResourcePool:\n    \"\"\"A single named resource with a fixed integer capacity.\n\n    The pool tracks ``used`` units in ``[0, capacity]``. :meth:`can_admit`\n    tests whether a cost would fit; :meth:`acquire` reserves it (raising if it\n    would overflow); :meth:`release` returns units. The pool also records the\n    peak usage ever reached so reporting can show how close a run came to\n    saturating it.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"name\", \"capacity\", \"used\", \"peak\")\n\n    def __init__(self, name, capacity):\n        if not isinstance(capacity, int) or capacity < 0:\n            raise ConfigError(\n                \"resource pool {!r} capacity must be a non-negative \"\n                \"integer\".format(name)\n            )\n        self.name = name\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.used = 0\n        self.peak = 0\n\n    def available(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the number of free units right now.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.capacity - self.used\n\n    def can_admit(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if ``cost`` units would fit without overflowing.\"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        return self.used + cost <= self.capacity\n\n    def acquire(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Reserve ``cost`` units, updating the peak. Raises if it won't fit.\n\n        Callers should gate this with :meth:`can_admit`; the guard here is a\n        safety net that enforces the never-over-admit invariant.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        if self.used + cost > self.capacity:\n            raise ResourceError(\n                \"pool {!r} would over-admit: used={} cost={} capacity={}\".format(\n                    self.name, self.used, cost, self.capacity\n                )\n            )\n        self.used += cost\n        if self.used > self.peak:\n            self.peak = self.used\n\n    def release(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Return ``cost`` units to the pool.\n\n        The amount released must equal what was acquired for the finishing\n        task; releasing more than is currently held is a bug in the caller and\n        is rejected rather than silently clamped, because a negative usage\n        would corrupt every later admission decision.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        if cost > self.used:\n            raise ResourceError(\n                \"pool {!r} would release more than held: used={} \"\n                \"release={}\".format(self.name, self.used, cost)\n            )\n        self.used -= 1\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the pool to empty, keeping its recorded peak.\"\"\"\n\n        self.used = 0\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"ResourcePool(name={!r}, used={}/{}, peak={})\".format(\n            self.name, self.used, self.capacity, self.peak\n        )\n\n\nclass ResourceManager:\n    \"\"\"Admission control across a set of named pools.\n\n    Construct from a ``{name: capacity}`` mapping. The manager exposes a single\n    all-or-nothing :meth:`try_admit` that reserves a whole cost mapping if and\n    only if every pool can satisfy its share, and a symmetric :meth:`release`\n    that returns exactly those units.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, capacities=None):\n        self._pools = {}\n        for name, capacity in (capacities or {}).items():\n            self._pools[name] = ResourcePool(name, capacity)\n\n    def pool(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the named pool, creating an unbounded-by-error miss guard.\"\"\"\n\n        if name not in self._pools:\n            raise ResourceError(\"unknown resource pool: {!r}\".format(name))\n        return self._pools[name]\n\n    def has_pool(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if a pool with ``name`` exists.\"\"\"\n\n        return name in self._pools\n\n    def can_admit(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every pool in ``costs`` can satisfy its cost.\n\n        A cost mapping that references an unknown pool is rejected. An empty or\n        ``None`` cost mapping always admits (the task uses no managed\n        resource).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not costs:\n            return True\n        for name, cost in costs.items():\n            if cost <= 0:\n                continue\n            if name not in self._pools:\n                raise ResourceError(\n                    \"unknown resource pool: {!r}\".format(name)\n                )\n            if not self._pools[name].can_admit(cost):\n                return False\n        return True\n\n    def try_admit(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Atomically reserve ``costs`` across all pools.\n\n        Returns ``True`` and reserves every unit if admission succeeds, or\n        returns ``False`` and reserves nothing if any pool cannot fit its\n        share. There is never a partial reservation.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not self.can_admit(costs):\n            return False\n        if costs:\n            for name, cost in costs.items():\n                if cost > 0:\n                    self._pools[name].acquire(cost)\n        return True\n\n    def release(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Release ``costs`` back to their pools (the inverse of admission).\n\n        The amounts must match what was acquired for the finishing task -- the\n        engine always releases the task's own ``resource_costs`` -- so each\n        pool gives back exactly what it lent.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not costs:\n            return\n        for name, cost in costs.items():\n            if cost > 0:\n                self._pools[name].release(cost)\n\n    def usage(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the current used units of a pool.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.pool(name).used\n\n    def peak_usage(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the peak used units a pool ever reached.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.pool(name).peak\n\n    def snapshot(self):\n        \"\"\"Return a ``{name: (used, capacity)}`` view of every pool.\"\"\"\n\n        return {\n            name: (pool.used, pool.capacity)\n            for name, pool in self._pools.items()\n        }\n\n    def pool_names(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the names of all managed pools in insertion order.\"\"\"\n\n        return list(self._pools.keys())\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"ResourceManager({})\".format(self.snapshot())\n\"\"\"A small synchronous publish/subscribe event bus.\n\nThe scheduler narrates everything it does as a stream of :class:`~taskflow.model.Event`\nobjects -- jobs starting, succeeding, failing, being skipped, resources being\nacquired and released. Observers (most importantly :class:`taskflow.history.History`)\nsubscribe to those events and build up whatever view they need.\n\nTwo kinds of subscription are supported:\n\n* **Exact** -- :meth:`EventBus.subscribe` matches one topic string exactly.\n* **Prefix** -- :meth:`EventBus.subscribe_prefix` matches a whole dotted\n  subtree. Subscribing to the prefix ``\"job.term\"`` receives ``\"job.term\"``\n  itself and every ``\"job.term.<something>\"`` such as ``\"job.term.failed\"`` and\n  ``\"job.term.skipped\"``, but not the unrelated ``\"job.started\"``.\n\nDelivery is synchronous, in-process, and ordered: every published event gets a\nmonotonically increasing sequence number, and handlers for a topic fire in the\norder they subscribed. There are no threads and no real time.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.model import Event\n\n\ndef _prefix_matches(prefix, topic):\n    \"\"\"Return ``True`` if ``topic`` lies in the dotted subtree of ``prefix``.\n\n    A topic matches a prefix when it equals the prefix exactly or when it is a\n    dotted descendant of it. The trailing dot is required so that the prefix\n    ``\"job\"`` matches ``\"job.started\"`` but not an unrelated ``\"jobless\"``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return topic == prefix or prefix.startswith(topic + \".\")\n\n\nclass Subscription:\n    \"\"\"A handle returned by ``subscribe`` that can later cancel delivery.\"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"bus\", \"kind\", \"key\", \"handler\", \"active\")\n\n    def __init__(self, bus, kind, key, handler):\n        self.bus = bus\n        self.kind = kind  # \"exact\" or \"prefix\"\n        self.key = key\n        self.handler = handler\n        self.active = True\n\n    def cancel(self):\n        \"\"\"Stop delivering events to this subscription. Idempotent.\"\"\"\n\n        if self.active:\n            self.bus._remove(self)\n            self.active = False\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"Subscription(kind={!r}, key={!r}, active={})\".format(\n            self.kind, self.key, self.active\n        )\n\n\nclass EventBus:\n    \"\"\"A synchronous, deterministic publish/subscribe bus.\n\n    Exact subscribers are stored per topic; prefix subscribers are stored in a\n    flat, insertion-ordered list and consulted on every publish. Each publish\n    stamps the event with the next sequence number and the supplied virtual\n    time, then delivers it -- first to the exact subscribers of its topic, then\n    to every matching prefix subscriber -- in subscription order.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self._exact = {}\n        self._prefix = []\n        self._seq = 0\n        self._published = []\n\n    # -- subscription -----------------------------------------------------\n\n    def subscribe(self, topic, handler):\n        \"\"\"Register ``handler`` for events published on exactly ``topic``.\n\n        Returns a :class:`Subscription`. The same handler may subscribe to\n        several topics; each call yields its own cancellable handle.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        sub = Subscription(self, \"exact\", topic, handler)\n        self._exact.setdefault(topic, []).append(sub)\n        return sub\n\n    def subscribe_prefix(self, prefix, handler):\n        \"\"\"Register ``handler`` for every event whose topic is under ``prefix``.\n\n        Returns a :class:`Subscription`. Prefix subscribers see the whole\n        dotted subtree rooted at ``prefix`` (see :func:`_prefix_matches`).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        sub = Subscription(self, \"prefix\", prefix, handler)\n        self._prefix.append(sub)\n        return sub\n\n    def _remove(self, sub):\n        \"\"\"Detach a subscription (called by :meth:`Subscription.cancel`).\"\"\"\n\n        if sub.kind == \"exact\":\n            bucket = self._exact.get(sub.key)\n            if bucket and sub in bucket:\n                bucket.remove(sub)\n                if not bucket:\n                    del self._exact[sub.key]\n        else:\n            if sub in self._prefix:\n                self._prefix.remove(sub)\n\n    # -- publishing -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def publish(self, topic, payload=None, at=0):\n        \"\"\"Publish an event on ``topic`` and deliver it synchronously.\n\n        A fresh :class:`~taskflow.model.Event` is created, stamped with the\n        next sequence number and the virtual time ``at``, appended to the bus's\n        own record, and handed to every matching handler. Returns the number of\n        handlers the event was delivered to.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        event = Event(topic=topic, payload=payload, seq=self._seq, at=at)\n        self._seq += 1\n        self._published.append(event)\n\n        delivered = 0\n        # Exact subscribers of this precise topic, in subscription order.\n        for sub in list(self._exact.get(topic, [])):\n            if sub.active:\n                sub.handler(event)\n                delivered += 1\n        # Prefix subscribers whose subtree contains this topic.\n        for sub in list(self._prefix):\n            if sub.active and _prefix_matches(sub.key, topic):\n                sub.handler(event)\n                delivered += 1\n        return delivered\n\n    # -- introspection    def topics(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the set of distinct topics that have been published.\"\"\"\n\n        return {event.topic for event in self._published}\n\n    def subscriber_count(self, The pool also records the\n    peak usage ever reached so reporting can show how close a run came to\n    saturating it.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"name\", \"capacity\", \"used\", \"peak\")\n\n    def __init__(self, name, capacity):\n        if not isinstance(capacity, int) or capacity < 0:\n            raise ConfigError(\n                \"resource pool {!r} capacity must be a non-negative \"\n                \"integer\".format(name)\n            )\n        self.name = name\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.used = 0\n        self.peak = 0\n\n    def available(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the number of free units right now.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.capacity - self.used\n\n    def can_admit(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if ``cost`` units would fit without overflowing.\"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        return self.used + cost <= self.capacity\n\n    def acquire(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Reserve ``cost`` units, updating the peak. Raises if it won't fit.\n\n        Callers should gate this with :meth:`can_admit`; the guard here is a\n        safety net that enforces the never-over-admit invariant.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        if self.used + cost > self.capacity:\n            raise ResourceError(\n                \"pool {!r} would over-admit: used={} cost={} capacity={}\".format(\n                    self.name, self.used, cost, self.capacity\n                )\n            )\n        self.used += cost\n        if self.used > self.peak:\n            self.peak = self.used\n\n    def release(self, cost):\n        \"\"\"Return ``cost`` units to the pool.\n\n        The amount released must equal what was acquired for the finishing\n        task; releasing more than is currently held is a bug in the caller and\n        is rejected rather than silently clamped, because a negative usage\n        would corrupt every later admission decision.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if cost < 0:\n            raise ResourceError(\"cost must be non-negative\")\n        if cost > self.used:\n            raise ResourceError(\n                \"pool {!r} would release more than held: used={} \"\n                \"release={}\".format(self.name, self.used, cost)\n            )\n        self.used -= 1\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the pool to empty, keeping its recorded peak.\"\"\"\n\n        self.used = 0\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"ResourcePool(name={!r}, used={}/{}, peak={})\".format(\n            self.name, self.used, self.capacity, self.peak\n        )\n\n\nclass ResourceManager:\n    \"\"\"Admission control across a set of named pools.\n\n    Construct from a ``{name: capacity}`` mapping. The manager exposes a single\n    all-or-nothing :meth:`try_admit` that reserves a whole cost mapping if and\n    only if every pool can satisfy its share, and a symmetric :meth:`release`\n    that returns exactly those units.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, capacities=None):\n        self._pools = {}\n        for name, capacity in (capacities or {}).items():\n            self._pools[name] = ResourcePool(name, capacity)\n\n    def pool(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the named pool, creating an unbounded-by-error miss guard.\"\"\"\n\n        if name not in self._pools:\n            raise ResourceError(\"unknown resource pool: {!r}\".format(name))\n        return self._pools[name]\n\n    def has_pool(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if a pool with ``name`` exists.\"\"\"\n\n        return name in self._pools\n\n    def can_admit(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` if every pool in ``costs`` can satisfy its cost.\n\n        A cost mapping that references an unknown pool is rejected. An empty or\n        ``None`` cost mapping always admits (the task uses no managed\n        resource).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not costs:\n            return True\n        for name, cost in costs.items():\n            if cost <= 0:\n                continue\n            if name not in self._pools:\n                raise ResourceError(\n                    \"unknown resource pool: {!r}\".format(name)\n                )\n            if not self._pools[name].can_admit(cost):\n                return False\n        return True\n\n    def try_admit(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Atomically reserve ``costs`` across all pools.\n\n        Returns ``True`` and reserves every unit if admission succeeds, or\n        returns ``False`` and reserves nothing if any pool cannot fit its\n        share. There is never a partial reservation.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not self.can_admit(costs):\n            return False\n        if costs:\n            for name, cost in costs.items():\n                if cost > 0:\n                    self._pools[name].acquire(cost)\n        return True\n\n    def release(self, costs):\n        \"\"\"Release ``costs`` back to their pools (the inverse of admission).\n\n        The amounts must match what was acquired for the finishing task -- the\n        engine always releases the task's own ``resource_costs`` -- so each\n        pool gives back exactly what it lent.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not costs:\n            return\n        for name, cost in costs.items():\n            if cost > 0:\n                self._pools[name].release(cost)\n\n    def usage(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the current used units of a pool.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.pool(name).used\n\n    def peak_usage(self, name):\n        \"\"\"Return the peak used units a pool ever reached.\"\"\"\n\n        return self.pool(name).peak\n\n    def snapshot(self):\n        \"\"\"Return a ``{name: (used, capacity)}`` view of every pool.\"\"\"\n\n        return {\n            name: (pool.used, pool.capacity)\n            for name, pool in self._pools.items()\n        }\n\n    def pool_names(self):\n        \"\"\"Return the names of all managed pools in insertion order.\"\"\"\n\n        return list(self._pools.keys())\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"ResourceManager({})\".format(self.snapshot())\n\"\"\"A small synchronous publish/subscribe event bus.\n\nThe scheduler narrates everything it does as a stream of :class:`~taskflow.model.Event`\nobjects -- jobs starting, succeeding, failing, being skipped, resources being\nacquired and released. Observers (most importantly :class:`taskflow.history.History`)\nsubscribe to those events and build up whatever view they need.\n\nTwo kinds of subscription are supported:\n\n* **Exact** -- :meth:`EventBus.subscribe` matches one topic string exactly.\n* **Prefix** -- :meth:`EventBus.subscribe_prefix` matches a whole dotted\n  subtree. Subscribing to the prefix ``\"job.term\"`` receives ``\"job.term\"``\n  itself and every ``\"job.term.<something>\"`` such as ``\"job.term.failed\"`` and\n  ``\"job.term.skipped\"``, but not the unrelated ``\"job.started\"``.\n\nDelivery is synchronous, in-process, and ordered: every published event gets a\nmonotonically increasing sequence number, and handlers for a topic fire in the\norder they subscribed. There are no threads and no real time.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom taskflow.model import Event\n\n\ndef _prefix_matches(prefix, topic):\n    \"\"\"Return ``True`` if ``topic`` lies in the dotted subtree of ``prefix``.\n\n    A topic matches a prefix when it equals the prefix exactly or when it is a\n    dotted descendant of it. The trailing dot is required so that the prefix\n    ``\"job\"`` matches ``\"job.started\"`` but not an unrelated ``\"jobless\"``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return topic == prefix or prefix.startswith(topic + \".\")\n\n\nclass Subscription:\n    \"\"\"A handle returned by ``subscribe`` that can later cancel delivery.\"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"bus\", \"kind\", \"key\", \"handler\", \"active\")\n\n    def __init__(self, bus, kind, key, handler):\n        self.bus = bus\n        self.kind = kind  # \"exact\" or \"prefix\"\n        self.key = key\n        self.handler = handler\n        self.active = True\n\n    def cancel(self):\n        \"\"\"Stop delivering events to this subscription. Idempotent.\"\"\"\n\n        if self.active:\n            self.bus._remove(self)\n            self.active = False\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return \"Subscription(kind={!r}, key={!r}, active={})\".format(\n            self.kind, self.key, self.active\n        )\n\n\nclass EventBus:\n    \"\"\"A synchronous, deterministic publish/subscribe bus.\n\n    Exact subscribers are stored per topic; prefix subscribers are stored in a\n    flat, insertion-ordered list and consulted on every publish. Each publish\n    stamps the event with the next sequence number and the supplied virtual\n    time, then delivers it -- first to the exact subscribers of its topic, then\n    to every matching prefix subscriber -- in subscription order.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self._exact = {}\n        self._prefix = []\n        self._seq = 0\n        self._published = []\n\n    # -- subscription -----------------------------------------------------\n\n    def subscribe(self, topic, handler):\n        \"\"\"Register ``handler`` for events published on exactly ``topic``.\n\n        Returns a :class:`Subscription`. The same handler may subscribe to\n        several topics; each call yields its own cancellable handle.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        sub = Subscription(self, \"exact\", topic, handler)\n        self._exact.setdefault(topic, []).append(sub)\n        return sub\n\n    def subscribe_prefix(self, prefix, handler):\n        \"\"\"Register ``handler`` for every event whose topic is under ``prefix``.\n\n        Returns a :class:`Subscription`. Prefix subscribers see the whole\n        dotted subtree rooted at ``prefix`` (see :func:`_prefix_matches`).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        sub = Subscription(self, \"prefix\", prefix, handler)\n        self._prefix.append(sub)\n        return sub\n\n    def _remove(self, sub):\n        \"\"\"Detach a subscription (called by :meth:`Subscription.cancel`).\"\"\"\n\n        if sub.kind == \"exact\":\n            bucket = self._exact.get(sub.key)\n            if bucket and sub in bucket:\n                bucket.remove(sub)\n                if not bucket:\n                    del self._exact[sub.key]\n        else:\n            if sub in self._prefix:\n                self._prefix.remove(sub)\n\n    # -- publishing -------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def publish(self, topic, payload=None, at=0):\n        \"\"\"Publish an event on ``topic`` and deliver it synchronously.\n\n        A fresh :class:`~taskflow.model.Event` is created, stamped with the\n        next sequence number and the virtual time ``at``, appended to the bus's\n        own record, and handed to every matching handler. Returns the number of\n        handlers the event was delivered to.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        event = Event(topic=topic, payload=payload, seq=self._seq, at=at)\n        self._seq += 1\n        self._published.append(event)\n\n        delivered = 0\n        # Exact subscribers of this precise topic, in subscription order.\n        for sub in list(self._exact.get(topic, [])):\n            if sub.active:\n                sub.handler(event)\n                delivered += 1\n        # It's now `< max_attempts`, which matches its docstring.\n3. **`taskflow/config.py`, `deep_merge`**: the recursive call had its arguments swapped, so pipeline defaults overwrote per-task settings (for example, a task's own `retry.max_attempts` was lost).
